The shift disrupted the structured routines he had cultivated for years and added evening events and cross‑time‑zone meetings that encroached on his art activities, hiking, and mentoring at the community center. Over the next year, colleagues saw his reliability and polished demeanor give way to disorganized scheduling, missed follow‑through on projects, and increasingly unusual comments during meetings, while at home his spouse noticed abrupt changes in his energy, sleep, and engagement with family and community events. After Thomas showed up unannounced at the office on a weekend insisting he needed to \"document a hidden pattern\" in company communications and his supervisor found him confused and unable to account for recent mistakes, HR contacted his spouse, who brought him to a psychiatric evaluation when it was clear his functioning had seriously deteriorated.\n- Chief Complaint: “I’ve been seeing and hearing things at work and at home that no one else seems to notice, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For months now I’ve been convinced that the emails and calendar invites going around the company are coded messages about me, like my new boss and people in other departments are quietly coordinating to test me or push me out, even when my wife and coworkers tell me it’s just normal office stuff.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): At the office and at home I keep hearing my name whispered from behind me and a man’s voice commenting on my work or warning me about “them,” and sometimes I see shadowy figures standing in the doorway or in the hallway that vanish when I look straight at them, and it’s gotten to the point where I can’t tell if I’m losing my mind or everyone else is missing something huge.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Outside of these high-energy spells, most days I just sit in my home office staring at the computer, feeling flat and blank, ignoring calls from friends and skipping the art group and hikes I used to love because I can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I’ll go from feeling on top of the world and weirdly giddy—like I’m the only one who really understands how everything connects—to snapping at my wife and coworkers over tiny things, slamming doors or firing off sharp emails because I suddenly feel like everyone is against me or wasting my time.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m in that revved-up mode, I’ll stay at my desk or pace the house all night starting spreadsheets, reorganizing files, sketching out big “communication systems” for the whole company and rewriting policies in my head, bouncing from task to task without actually finishing anything because I feel too wired to sit still.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are stretches where I honestly believe I’m the only person in the organization who can see the bigger pattern in all the communications, like I’ve been given this special ability to decode it and that senior leadership will eventually recognize me as the one who can fix everything if I just push hard enough.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): Lately I can go two or three nights sleeping only a couple of hours, sometimes just dozing in my chair, and I wake up feeling charged and sure I don’t really need more sleep because my mind is racing with ideas I’m convinced I have to act on right away.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking about what I’m noticing—like the supposed patterns in emails or how meetings are connected—I can’t seem to stop; I jump from one thought to the next so fast that people cut me off or look confused, but I feel this pressure in my chest to keep explaining before the ideas disappear.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I’m in those super-charged phases, barely sleeping and racing around with big plans for overhauling the company’s communication system, that’s also when the whispers get loudest and the shadowy figures seem to show up most, like the energy and the strange experiences are happening at the same time.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over this past year or so, I feel like I’ve spent more time revved up—talking fast, sleeping very little, chasing big ideas about my special role at work—than in the flatter, drained periods in between.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch earlier this year when my mood felt pretty neutral—no big highs or crashes—but I was still hearing my name whispered and was sure the emails were coded about me, even though my energy was low and I wasn’t buzzing with ideas like during my manic spells.\n- functional_impairment: Over the past several months I’ve missed deadlines, had projects taken away from me, stopped going to my art group and hikes, and my spouse has had to step in with HR and manage bills and appointments because I can’t keep my work or home responsibilities straight anymore.\n- duration: Ever since the reorganization about fifteen months ago, these swings in mood along with the strange experiences at work and home have been building and never really gone away.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: For roughly the past ten months, I’ve been dealing pretty steadily with the whispers, the shadowy figures, and the belief that company messages are secretly about me.\n- manic_episode_duration: For almost the whole past year, with only brief breaks, I’ve had these amped-up periods where I sleep very little, feel unusually important, and throw myself into grand plans for fixing the company’s systems.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's when my wife and coworkers tell me it’s just normal office stuff.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): At the office and at home I keep hearing my name whispered from behind me and a man’s voice commenting on my work or warning me about “them,” and sometimes I see shadowy figures standing in the doorway or in the hallway that vanish when I look straight at them, and it’s gotten to the point where I can’t tell if I’m losing my mind or everyone else is missing something huge.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Outside of these high-energy spells, most days I just sit in my home office staring at the computer, feeling flat and blank, ignoring calls from friends and skipping the art group and hikes I used to love because I can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I’ll go from feeling on top of the world and weirdly giddy—like I’m the only one who really understands how everything connects—to snapping at my wife and coworkers over tiny things, slamming doors or firing off sharp emails because I suddenly feel like everyone is against me or wasting my time.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m in that revved-up mode, I’ll stay at my desk or pace the house all night starting spreadsheets, reorganizing files, sketching out big “communication systems” for the whole company and rewriting policies in my head, bouncing from task to task without actually finishing anything because I feel too wired to sit still.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are stretches where I honestly believe I’m the only person in the organization who can see the bigger pattern in all the communications, like I’ve been given this special ability to decode it and that senior leadership will eventually recognize me as the one who can fix everything if I just push hard enough.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): Lately I can go two or three nights sleeping only a couple of hours, sometimes just dozing in my chair, and I wake up feeling charged and sure I don’t really need more sleep because my mind is racing with ideas I’m convinced I have to act on right away.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking about what I’m noticing—like chest to keep explaining before the ideas disappear.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I’m in those super-charged phases, barely sleeping and racing around with big plans for overhauling the company’s communication system, that’s also when the whispers get loudest and the shadowy figures seem to show up most, like the energy and the strange experiences are happening at the same time.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over the past several months I’ve missed deadlines, had projects taken away from me, stopped going to my art group and hikes, and my spouse has had to step in with HR and manage bills and appointments because I can’t keep my work or home responsibilities straight anymore.\n- duration: Ever since the reorganization about fifteen months ago, these swings in mood along with the strange experiences at hearing things at work and at home that no one else seems to notice, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For months now I’ve been convinced that the emails and calendar invites going around the company are coded messages about me, like my new boss and people in other departments are quietly coordinating to test me or push me out, even when my wife and coworkers tell me it’s just normal office stuff.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): At the office and at home I keep hearing my name whispered from behind me and a man’s voice commenting on my work or warning me about “them,” and sometimes I see shadowy figures standing in the doorway or in the hallway that vanish when I look straight at them, and it’s gotten to the point where I can’t tell if I’m losing my mind or everyone else is missing something huge.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Outside of these high-energy spells, most days I just sit in my home office staring at the computer, feeling flat and blank, ignoring calls from friends and skipping the art group and hikes I used to love because I can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I’ll go from feeling on top of the world and weirdly giddy—like I’m the only one who really understands how everything connects—to snapping at my wife and coworkers over tiny things, slamming doors or firing off sharp emails because I suddenly feel like everyone is against me or wasting my time.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m in that revved-up mode, I’ll stay at my desk or pace the house all night starting spreadsheets, reorganizing files, sketching out big “communication systems” for the whole company and rewriting policies in my head, bouncing from task who can see the bigger pattern in all the communications, like I’ve been given this special ability to decode it and that senior leadership will eventually recognize me as the one who can fix everything if I just push hard enough.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): Lately I can go two or three nights sleeping only a couple of hours, sometimes just dozing in my chair, and I wake up feeling charged and sure I don’t really need more sleep because my mind is racing with ideas I’m convinced I have to act on right away.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking about what I’m noticing—like the supposed patterns in emails or how meetings are connected—I can’t seem to stop; I jump from one thought to the next so fast that people cut me off or look confused, but I feel this pressure in my chest to keep explaining before the ideas disappear.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I’m in those super-charged phases, barely sleeping and racing around with big plans for overhauling the company’s communication system, that’s also when the whispers get loudest and the shadowy figures seem to show up most, like the energy and the strange experiences are happening at the same time.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over this past year or so, I feel like I’ve spent more time revved up—talking fast, sleeping very little, chasing big ideas about my special role at work—than in the flatter, drained periods in between.\n- sleeping and racing around with big plans for overhauling the company’s communication system, that’s also when the whispers get loudest and the shadowy figures seem to show up most, like the energy and the strange experiences are happening at the same time.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over Even with my mood being neutral, I was still hearing the whispers and believing those company emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the entire ten-month period, have you experienced any episodes of significant depression or mania that lasted for at least two weeks at a time, or were the mood changes mostly related to your high-energy, 'amped up' states?\n\nPatient: Looking back, my mood changes have been almost completely dominated by those high-energy, amped-up states, which have lasted for weeks and months at a time. I've spent the vast majority of the past year in that revved-up phase—barely sleeping and chasing grand plans—with only brief breaks or occasional flat periods in between. I haven't really had any major depressive crashes, just those constant highs and the occasional flat spell.\n\nDoctor: During those months when you were 'amped up,' did you experience a persistent period of at least one week where you needed very little sleep, felt unusually energetic, and engaged in risky or highly impulsive activities?"

and sure I don’t really need more sleep because my mind is racing with ideas I’m convinced I have to act on right away.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking about what I’m noticing—like The shift disrupted the structured routines he had cultivated for years and added evening events and cross‑time‑zone meetings that encroached on his art activities, hiking, and mentoring at the community center. Over the next year, colleagues saw his reliability and polished demeanor give way to disorganized scheduling, missed follow‑through on projects, and increasingly unusual comments during meetings, while at home his spouse noticed abrupt changes in his energy, sleep, and engagement with family and community events. After Thomas showed up unannounced at the office on a weekend insisting he needed to \"document a hidden pattern\" in company communications and his supervisor found him confused and unable to account for recent mistakes, HR contacted his spouse, who brought him to a psychiatric evaluation when it was clear his functioning had seriously deteriorated.\n- Chief Complaint: “I’ve been seeing and hearing things at work and at home that no one else seems to notice, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For months now I’ve been convinced when my wife and coworkers tell me it’s just normal office stuff.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): At the office and at home I keep hearing my name whispered from behind me and a man’s voice commenting on my work or warning me about “them,” and sometimes I see shadowy figures standing in the doorway or in the hallway that vanish when I look straight at them, and it’s gotten to the point where I can’t tell if I’m losing my mind or everyone else is missing something huge.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Outside of these high-energy spells, most days I just sit in my home office staring at the computer, feeling flat and blank, ignoring calls from friends and skipping the art group and hikes I used to love because I can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I’ll go from feeling on top of the world and weirdly giddy—like I’m the only one who really understands how everything connects—to snapping at my wife and coworkers over tiny things, slamming doors or firing off sharp emails because I suddenly feel like everyone is against me or wasting my time.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m in that revved-up mode, I’ll stay at my desk or pace the house all night starting spreadsheets, reorganizing files, sketching out big “communication systems” for the whole company and rewriting policies in my head, bouncing from task to task without actually finishing anything because I feel too wired to sit still.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are stretches where I honestly believe I’m the only person in the organization who can see the bigger pattern in all the communications, like I’ve been given this special ability to decode it and that senior leadership will eventually recognize me as the one who can fix everything if I just push hard enough.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): Lately I can go two or three nights sleeping only a couple of hours, sometimes just dozing in my chair, and I wake up feeling charged and sure I don’t really need more sleep because my mind is racing with ideas I’m convinced I have to act on right away.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking about what I’m noticing—like the supposed patterns in emails or how meetings are connected—I can’t seem to stop; I jump from one thought to the next so fast that people cut me off or look confused, but I feel this pressure in my chest to keep explaining before the ideas disappear.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I’m in those super-charged phases, barely sleeping and racing around with big plans for overhauling the company’s communication system, that’s also when the whispers get loudest and the shadowy figures seem to show up most, like the energy and the strange experiences are happening at the same time.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over this past year or so, I feel like I’ve spent more time revved up—talking fast, sleeping very little, chasing big ideas about my special role at work—than in the flatter, drained periods in between.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch earlier this year when my mood felt pretty neutral—no big highs or crashes—but I was still hearing my name whispered and was sure the emails were coded about me, even though my energy was low and I wasn’t buzzing with ideas like during my manic spells.\n- functional_impairment: Over the past several months I’ve missed deadlines, had projects taken away from me, stopped going to my art group and hikes, and my spouse has had to step in with HR and manage bills and appointments because I can’t keep my work or home responsibilities straight anymore.\n- duration: Ever since the reorganization about fifteen months ago, these swings in mood along with the strange experiences at work and home have been building and never really gone away.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: For roughly the past ten months, I’ve been dealing pretty steadily with the whispers, the shadowy figures, and the belief that company messages are secretly about me.\n- manic_episode_duration: For almost the whole past year, with only brief breaks, I’ve had these amped-up periods where I sleep very Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Dr. Smith. I’ve been seeing and hearing things at work and at home that no one else seems to notice, and it’s starting to scare me. It has gotten to the point where I'm having a hard time telling what is actually real.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these sights and sounds, and have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling unusually depressed or manic, during this same time?\n\nPatient: I've been hearing the whispers and seeing those shadowy figures for about ten months now. During this time, my mood has been swinging between flat, empty periods where I don't care about anything, and these highly wired, irritable phases where I feel completely amped up. The sights and sounds actually get much worse and more intense when I'm in those high-energy moods.\n\nDoctor: During the periods when you feel 'amped up' or flat, do these mood changes last for a significant portion of the total time you've been experiencing the hallucinations, or are they relatively brief compared to the overall ten months?\n\nPatient: Yes, those amped-up periods have actually lasted for the vast majority of this past year. I've spent most of my time feeling completely revved up, with only very brief breaks or flatter periods in between. It definitely hasn't been a short phase; it's been going on like that almost constantly.\n\nDoctor: During those 'amped up' periods, did the hallucinations and delusions occur only during the times when your mood was elevated, or did they persist even when your mood was relatively stable?\n\nPatient: They definitely get loudest when I'm amped up, but they also persisted during times when my mood felt completely stable. Earlier this year, there was a stretch of a few weeks where I felt pretty neutral and wasn't buzzing with energy, but I was still hearing the whispers and believing the emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: During that period of a few weeks when your mood felt stable, did you experience any symptoms of depression, such as feeling hopeless, losing interest in activities, or having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: During that stretch, my mood felt pretty neutral and stable, so I didn't feel deeply depressed or hopeless. I did feel a bit flat and unmotivated—like just staring at my computer and skipping my art group—but it wasn't a major depressive crash. Even with my mood being neutral, I was still hearing the whispers and believing those company emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the entire ten-month period, have you experienced any episodes of significant depression or mania that lasted for at least two weeks at a time, or were the mood changes mostly related to your high-energy, 'amped up' states?\n\nPatient: Looking back, my mood changes have been almost completely dominated by those high-energy, amped-up states, which have lasted for weeks and months at a time. I've spent the vast majority of the past year in that revved-up phase—barely sleeping and chasing grand plans—with only brief breaks or occasional flat periods in between. I haven't really had any major depressive crashes, just those constant highs and the occasional flat spell.\n\nDoctor: During those months when you were 'amped up,' did you experience a persistent period of at least one week where you needed very little sleep, felt unusually energetic, and engaged in risky or highly impulsive activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, those periods lasted much longer than a week and would go on for months at a time. I’d regularly go two or three nights sleeping only a couple of hours but still wake up feeling completely charged and wired. While I didn't do anything wild or dangerous, my impulsivity showed up as staying up all night pacing, starting massive spreadsheets, and obsessively trying to rewrite company policies without ever finishing them.\n\nDoctor: Over the entire ten-month period, were there any stretches of at least two weeks where you experienced the hallucinations and delusions without any significant mood symptoms present?\n\nPatient: Yes, earlier this year there was a stretch of a few weeks where my mood felt completely neutral and stable, with no big highs or crashes. Even though I didn't have that buzzing energy, I was still hearing the whispers and remained convinced that the company emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: During that multi-week period where your mood was stable and you were not feeling 'amped up,' did you experience any significant functional impairment, such as difficulty maintaining your job responsibilities or managing your daily self-care?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when my mood was stable, the whispers and my belief about the coded emails made it impossible to focus, causing me to miss deadlines and lose projects. It got to the point where my wife had to step in to manage our household bills and daily responsibilities because I couldn't keep things straight. Those experiences alone were enough to severely disrupt my ability to function, even without the high energy.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, did you experience these psychotic symptoms (the whispers and the belief about coded emails) for at least two weeks in the absence of any prominent mood symptoms, like the high energy or the flat spells, at any other point during these ten months?"

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Dr. Smith. I’ve been seeing and hearing things at work and at home that no one else seems to notice, and it’s starting to scare me. It has gotten to the point where I'm having a hard time telling what is actually real.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these sights and sounds, and have you noticed any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling unusually depressed or manic, during this same time?\n\nPatient: I've been hearing the whispers and seeing those shadowy figures for about ten months now. During this time, my mood has been swinging between flat, empty periods where I don't care about anything, and these highly wired, irritable phases where I feel completely amped up. The sights and sounds actually get much worse and more intense when I'm in those high-energy moods.\n\nDoctor: During the periods when you feel 'amped up' or flat, do these mood changes last for a significant portion of the total time you've been experiencing the hallucinations, or are they relatively brief compared to the overall ten months?\n\nPatient: Yes, those amped-up periods have actually lasted for the vast majority of this past year. I've spent most of my time feeling completely revved up, with only very brief breaks or flatter periods in between. It definitely hasn't been a short phase; it's been going on like that almost constantly.\n\nDoctor: During those 'amped up' periods, did the hallucinations and delusions occur only during the times when your mood was elevated, or did they persist even when your mood was relatively stable?\n\nPatient: They definitely get loudest when I'm amped up, but they also persisted during times when my mood felt completely stable. Earlier this year, there was a stretch of a few weeks where I felt pretty neutral and wasn't buzzing with energy, but I was still hearing the whispers and believing the emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: During that period of a few weeks when your mood felt stable, did you experience any symptoms of depression, such as feeling hopeless, losing interest in activities, or having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: During that stretch, my mood felt pretty neutral and stable, so I didn't feel deeply depressed or hopeless. I did feel a bit flat and unmotivated—like just staring at my computer and skipping my art group—but it wasn't a major depressive crash. Even with my mood being neutral, I was still hearing the whispers and believing those company emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the entire ten-month period, have you experienced any episodes of significant depression or mania that lasted for at least two weeks at a time, or were the mood changes mostly related to your high-energy, 'amped up' states?\n\nPatient: Looking back, my mood changes have been almost completely dominated by those high-energy, amped-up states, which have lasted for weeks and months at a time. I've spent the vast majority of the past year in that revved-up phase—barely sleeping and chasing grand plans—with only brief breaks or occasional flat periods in between. I haven't really had any major depressive crashes, just those constant highs and the occasional flat spell.\n\nDoctor: During those months when you were 'amped up,' did you experience a persistent period of at least one week where you needed very little sleep, felt unusually energetic, and engaged in risky or highly impulsive activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, those periods lasted much longer than a week and would go on for months at a time. I’d regularly go two or three nights sleeping only a couple of hours but still wake up feeling completely charged and wired. Even though I didn't have that buzzing energy, I was still hearing the whispers and remained convinced that the company emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: During that multi-week period where your mood was stable and you were not feeling 'amped up,' did you experience any significant functional impairment, such as difficulty maintaining your job responsibilities or managing your daily self-care?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when my mood was stable, the whispers and my belief about the coded emails made it impossible to focus, causing me to miss deadlines and lose projects. It got to the point where my wife had to step in to manage our household bills and daily responsibilities because I couldn't keep things straight. Those experiences alone were enough to severely disrupt my ability to function, even without the high energy.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, did you experience these psychotic symptoms (the whispers and the belief about coded emails) for at least two weeks in the absence of any prominent mood symptoms, like the high energy or the flat spells, at any other point during these ten months?\n\nPatient: No, it was primarily during that one stretch of a few weeks earlier this year where my mood felt completely neutral. Even though I wasn't experiencing the highs or the flat spells then, the whispers and the belief about the coded emails were still happening every day.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any period of at least two weeks where you had the whispers and the belief about the emails while your mood was stable, prior to the onset of the high-energy states you described?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was indeed a stretch of a few weeks earlier this year where my mood was completely neutral and I had no high-energy spells or crashes. Even though I wasn't experiencing those buzzing manic states, I was still hearing the whispers and remained convinced the emails were coded messages about me. That lasted for at least two weeks during that stable period.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the very beginning of these ten months, did the hallucinations and delusions start before you ever experienced any of these 'amped up' or high-energy mood episodes?"

I've spent most of my time feeling completely revved up, with only very brief breaks or flatter periods in between. It definitely hasn't been a short phase; it's been going on like that almost constantly.\n\nDoctor: During those 'amped up' periods, did the hallucinations and delusions occur only during the times when your mood was elevated, or did they persist even when your mood was relatively stable?\n\nPatient: They definitely get loudest when I'm amped up, but they also persisted during times when my mood felt completely stable. Earlier this year, there was a stretch of a few weeks where I felt pretty neutral and wasn't buzzing with energy, but I was still hearing the whispers and believing the emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: During that period of a few weeks when your mood felt stable, did you experience any symptoms of depression, such as feeling hopeless, losing interest in activities, or having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: During that stretch, my mood felt pretty neutral and stable, so I didn't feel deeply depressed or hopeless. I did feel a bit flat and unmotivated—like just staring at my computer and skipping my art group—but it wasn't a major depressive crash. Even with my mood being neutral, I was still hearing the whispers and believing those company emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the entire ten-month period, have you experienced any episodes of significant depression or mania that lasted for at least two weeks at a time, or were the mood changes mostly related to your high-energy, 'amped up' states?\n\nPatient: Looking back, my mood changes have been almost completely dominated by those high-energy, amped-up states, which have lasted for weeks and months at a time. I've spent the vast majority of the past year in that revved-up phase—barely sleeping and chasing grand plans—with only brief breaks or occasional flat periods in between. I haven't really had any major depressive crashes, just those constant highs and the occasional flat spell.\n\nDoctor: During those months when you were 'amped up,' did you experience a persistent period of at least one week where you needed very little sleep, felt unusually energetic, and engaged in risky or highly impulsive activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, those periods lasted much longer than a week and would go on for months at a time. I’d regularly go two or three nights sleeping only a couple of hours but still wake up feeling completely charged and wired. While I didn't do anything wild or dangerous, my impulsivity showed up as staying up all night pacing, starting massive spreadsheets, and obsessively trying to rewrite company policies without ever finishing them.\n\nDoctor: Over the entire ten-month period, were there any stretches of at least two weeks where you experienced the hallucinations and delusions without any significant mood symptoms present?\n\nPatient: Yes, earlier this year there was a stretch of a few weeks where my mood felt completely neutral and stable, with no big highs or crashes. Even though I didn't have that buzzing energy, I was still hearing the whispers and remained convinced that the company emails were coded messages about me.\n\nDoctor: During that multi-week period where your mood was stable and you were not feeling 'amped up,' did you experience any significant functional impairment, such as difficulty maintaining your job responsibilities or managing your daily self-care?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when my mood was stable, the whispers and my belief about the coded emails made it impossible to focus, causing me to miss deadlines and lose projects. It got to the point where my wife had to step in to manage our household bills and daily responsibilities because I couldn't keep things straight. Those experiences alone were enough to severely disrupt my ability to function, even without the high energy.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, did you experience these psychotic symptoms (the whispers and the belief about coded emails) for at least two weeks in the absence of any prominent mood symptoms, like the high energy or the flat spells, at any other point during these ten months?\n\nPatient: No, it was primarily during that one stretch of a few weeks earlier this year where my mood felt completely neutral. Even though I wasn't experiencing the highs or the flat spells then, the whispers and the belief about the coded emails were still happening every day.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any period of at least two weeks where you had the whispers and the belief about the emails while your mood was stable, prior to the onset of the high-energy states you described?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was indeed a stretch of a few weeks earlier this year where my mood was completely neutral and I had no high-energy spells or crashes. Even though I wasn't experiencing those buzzing manic states, I was still hearing the whispers and remained convinced the emails were coded messages about me. That lasted for at least two weeks during that stable period.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the very beginning of these ten months, did the hallucinations and delusions start before you ever experienced any of these 'amped up' or high-energy mood episodes?\n\nPatient: Actually, looking back, the high-energy \"amped up\" periods started first, about a year ago, while the whispering and shadowy figures didn't become a steady presence until about ten months ago. Both have been building up since the reorganization fifteen months ago, but the mood swings definitely seemed to happen before the hallucinations and delusions."

Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt. Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
